Output:
Optimized parameters
\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{K}

To provide a rigorous foundation for our proposed framework, we first analyze two baseline variants in the Appendix:
Algorithm

2
, which establishes the core switching logic with single local update
E=1
under full participation
m=n
, and
Algorithm

3
, which extends this logic to multiple local steps
E\geq 1
with full participation
m=n
. These baseline analyses serve as the theoretical stepping stones for our primary theoretical contribution presented in the main text for
Algorithm

1
, which addresses the more general scenario of partial participation
m\leq n
with multiple local updates
E\geq 1
.

Appendix B
Lemmas Used in Proofs

B.1
Three-point Bregman Divergence Identity, see equation (4.1) on page 297 of
[
Bubeck et al. 2015
]
.

Let
\psi
be a convex function, then for Bregman divergence
D_{\psi}[\mathbf{x}||\mathbf{x}^{\prime}]:=\psi(\mathbf{x})-\psi(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\langle\nabla\psi(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\rangle\geq 0
with any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
in the domain of
\psi
,
the following identity holds for any three points
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\hat{\mathbf{x}}
in the domain of
\psi
:

\langle\nabla\psi(\mathbf{x})-\nabla\psi(\hat{\mathbf{x}}),\hat{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\rangle=D_{\psi}[\mathbf{x}^{\prime}||\mathbf{x}]-D_{\psi}[\mathbf{x}^{\prime}||\hat{\mathbf{x}}]-D_{\psi}[\hat{\mathbf{x}}||\mathbf{x}]

B.2
Polarization Identity.

The update direction
\mathbf{u}_{k}
and the parameters
\mathbf{w}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k+1}
in the update rule
\mathbf{w}_{k+1}=\mathbf{w}_{k}-\eta\mathbf{u}_{k}
with step size
\eta>0
satisfies the following identity:

\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left(\eta^{2}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|^{2}-\|\mathbf{w}_{k+1}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|^{2}\right)

B.3
Properties of Weighted Functions.

F(\mathbf{w})
,
G(\mathbf{w})
and
F(\mathbf{w};\mathcal{I}_{k}),G(\mathbf{w},\mathcal{I}_{k})
are:

•

Convex on
\Theta
, if all components of
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w})
and
\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w})
are convex on
\Theta
.

•

L
-Lipschitz continuous on
\Theta
, if all components of
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w})
and
\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w})
are
L
-Lipschitz continuous on
\Theta
.

B.4
Properties of Softmax Mean.

Let the softmax mean be defined as

m(\mathbf{x},\alpha):=\langle\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{x}),\mathbf{x}\rangle=\frac{\mathbf{x}^{\top}\exp(\alpha\mathbf{x})}{\bm{1}^{\top}\exp(\alpha\mathbf{x})}

where
\mathbf{x}=([\mathbf{x}]_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\alpha\geq 0
.
The softmax mean
m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)
with hyperparameter
\alpha
satisfies:

\displaystyle m(\mathbf{x}+C\bm{1},\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)+C,\quad\forall C\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle m(C\mathbf{x},\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\cdot m(\mathbf{x},C\alpha),\quad\forall C\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle m(\mathbf{x},\alpha^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle m(\mathbf{x},\alpha),\quad\text{for }\alpha^{\prime}\geq\alpha

Moreover, the following inequality holds:

0\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)=\lim_{\alpha\to\infty}m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)-m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)<k,\quad\text{for }\alpha\geq\underline{\alpha}:=\frac{\ln n}{k},k>0

B.5
Properties of Masked Softmax Mean.

Let the masked softmax mean with a nonempty subset
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{I}
be

m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha):=\langle\mathrm{softmax}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\alpha\mathbf{x}),\mathbf{x}\rangle=\frac{\mathbf{x}^{\top}(\bm{1}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\odot\exp(\alpha\mathbf{x}))}{\bm{1}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}^{\top}\exp(\alpha\mathbf{x})}=\frac{\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}^{\top}\exp(\alpha\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}})}{\bm{1}^{\top}\exp(\alpha\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}})}=\langle\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}),\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\rangle=m(\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}},\alpha)

where
\mathbf{x}=([\mathbf{x}]_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbf{x}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}=([\mathbf{x}]_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}=(x_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
\alpha\geq 0
and the masked softmax operator
\mathrm{softmax}_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
is defined in
Eq.

10
.
The masked softmax mean
m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha)
with hyperparameter
\alpha
satisfies:

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}+C\bm{1},\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha)+C,\quad\forall C\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(C\mathbf{x},\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\cdot m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},C\alpha),\quad\forall C\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha),\quad\text{for }\alpha^{\prime}\geq\alpha

Moreover, the following inequality holds:

0\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha)=\lim_{\alpha\to\infty}m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha)-m_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\alpha)<k,\quad\text{for }\alpha\geq\underline{\alpha}:=\frac{\ln m}{k},k>0

B.6
Deviation Bound of Softmax Mean.

Let
\mathbf{x}=([\mathbf{x}]_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\bm{\delta}=([\bm{\delta}]_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}=(\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

and
\alpha\geq 0
, then the deviation bound of
\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\langle\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha[\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta}]),\mathbf{x}\rangle
satisfies:

\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\langle\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha[\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta}]),\mathbf{x}\rangle\leq 2\|\bm{\delta}\|_{\infty}+k,\quad\text{for }\alpha\geq\underline{\alpha}:=\frac{\ln n}{k},k>0

m(\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta},\alpha)-\langle\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha[\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta}]),\mathbf{x}\rangle\leq\|\bm{\delta}\|_{\infty}.

Proof for
Lemma

B.4
.

The first two properties are straightforward to verify by the definition of softmax mean.
For the third non-decreasing property, it is due to the non-negativity of the partial derivative with respect to
\alpha
.
By letting
\mathbf{p}(\alpha):=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{x})
, then:

\frac{\partial m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)}{\partial\alpha}=\mathbf{x}^{\top}(\mathrm{diag}(\mathbf{p}(\alpha))-\mathbf{p}(\alpha)\mathbf{p}(\alpha)^{\top})\mathbf{x}=\mathrm{Var}_{\mathbf{p}(\alpha)}[\mathbf{x}]\geq 0

The left hand side of thelast inequality follows from the definition of softmax mean.

\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}=\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\bm{1}\rangle\geq\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\mathbf{x}\rangle=m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)

Let’s focus on the right hand side of the last inequality.

Since the inequality holds when
n=1
, we only need to prove the inequality for
n>1
.
Letting
\mathbf{p}(\alpha):=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{x})
, then by applying Jensen’s inequality to the concave function
\ln(\cdot)
,
we derive the upper bound of the entropy of
\mathbf{p}(\alpha)
:

\mathrm{Ent}[\mathbf{p}(\alpha)]:=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}(\alpha)]_{i}\ln\frac{1}{[\mathbf{p}(\alpha)]_{i}}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{p}(\alpha)}\ln\frac{1}{\mathbf{p}(\alpha)}\leq\ln\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{p}(\alpha)}\frac{1}{\mathbf{p}(\alpha)}=\ln n

By the definition of softmax mean
m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{p}(\alpha)}[\mathbf{x}]
, then for the case
n>1
, we have:

\mathrm{Ent}[\mathbf{p}(\alpha)]=-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{p}(\alpha)}[\alpha\mathbf{x}]+\ln\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\exp(\alpha[\mathbf{x}]_{i})>-\alpha\cdot m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)+\alpha\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}

Combining these two inequalities, when
\alpha\geq\frac{\ln n}{k}>0
for some
k>0,n>1
, we have:

\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)<\frac{\ln n}{\alpha}\leq k

∎

Proof for
Lemma

B.6
.

Let
\mathbf{p}(\alpha):=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha(\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta}))
and
\mathbf{z}:=\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta}
,
by using the last inequality in
Lemma

B.4
, we have:

m(\mathbf{z},\alpha)=\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\mathbf{z}\rangle\geq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{z}]_{i}-\frac{\ln n}{\alpha}.

Substituting
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta}
, and using
\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{z}]_{i}-\min_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\bm{\delta}]_{i}\geq\max_{i\in\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}
gives

\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta}\rangle\geq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}+\min_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\bm{\delta}]_{i}-\frac{\ln n}{\alpha}

Rearranging and using
\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\bm{\delta}\rangle-\min_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\bm{\delta}]\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\bm{\delta}]_{i}-\min_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\bm{\delta}]_{i}\leq 2\|\bm{\delta}\|_{\infty}

and
\alpha\geq\frac{\ln n}{k},k>0
yields

\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\mathbf{x}\rangle\leq 2\|\bm{\delta}\|_{\infty}+\frac{\ln n}{\alpha}\leq 2\|\bm{\delta}\|_{\infty}+k.

This completes the proof of the first inequality.
Regarding the second inequality, we again apply the definition of softmax mean and the inequality
\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\bm{\delta}\rangle\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\bm{\delta}]_{i}\leq\|\bm{\delta}\|_{\infty}
and
\alpha\geq\frac{\ln n}{k},k>0
to get:

m(\mathbf{x}+\bm{\delta},\alpha)-\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\mathbf{x}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{p}(\alpha),\bm{\delta}\rangle\leq\|\bm{\delta}\|_{\infty}.

This completes the proof of the second inequality.
∎

B.7
Subgaussianity.

Suppose a random variable
X
such that

\mathbb{E}[X]=0
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(X^{2})]\leq 2
, then
Z
is 1-subgaussian, i.e.,

\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda X)]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R}

.

Suppose a random vector
\mathbf{z}
such that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}\mid\mathcal{F}]=\vec{0}

and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}]\leq 2
, then
\mathbf{z}
is
\sigma^{2}
-subgaussian, i.e.,

\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\langle\mathbf{e},\mathbf{z}\rangle)\mid\mathcal{F}]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}\quad\forall\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R}

since by letting
X=\|z\|/\sigma
and using the above lemma, we have:

\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\langle\mathbf{e},\mathbf{z}\rangle)\mid\mathcal{F}]\leq\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\|\mathbf{z}\|)\mid\mathcal{F}]=\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\sigma\|\mathbf{z}\|/\sigma)\mid\mathcal{F}]=\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp((\lambda\sigma)X)\mid\mathcal{F}]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}

B.8
Average of Subgaussian Random Vectors.

Suppose random vectors
(\mathbf{z}_{\tau})_{\tau\in[E]}
and a filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{\tau})_{\tau\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

are such that
\mathbf{z}_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau+1}
-measurable and

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\vec{0},\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq 2
for all
\tau\in[E]
,
then the average of the random vectors
\mathbf{z}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{E}}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{\tau}

satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]=\vec{0}
and the following subgaussian tail bound:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{1}{4E}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\right\|^{2}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}\right]\leq 2

Remark 1 for
Lemma

B.8
.

Let
(\mathcal{F}_{s})_{s=1}^{B}
be a filtration, and
\mathcal{D}

be a distribution on a measurable space
\mathcal{Z}
.
Let a sequence
(\zeta_{s})_{s=1}^{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{D}
be drawn from
\mathcal{D}
independently and identically,
and is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
, i.e.,
(\zeta_{s})_{s=1}^{B}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\mathcal{F}_{0}
.
The filtration is generated sequentially such that
\mathcal{F}_{s}=\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{s-1},\zeta_{s})
,
and
\mathbf{w}_{0}\in\Theta
is
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-measurable.
Then for a measurable function
\mathbf{h}:\Theta\times\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
assume that for any deterministic
\mathbf{w}\in\Theta
,
we have
\mathbb{E}_{\zeta\sim\mathcal{D}}[\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w},\zeta)]=\vec{0}

and
\mathbb{E}_{\zeta\sim\mathcal{D}}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w},\zeta)\|^{2})]\leq 2
.
Then for
\mathbf{z}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{B}}\sum_{s=1}^{B}\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w}_{0},\zeta_{s})
,
it satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]=\vec{0}
and a subgaussian bound:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]\leq 2

Remark 2 for
Lemma

B.8
.

Let
(\mathcal{F}_{\tau})_{\tau\in[E]}
be a filtration, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}^{\otimes B}

be a distribution on a measurable space
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Z}^{B}
.
Let a sequence
(\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})_{\tau\in[E]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be drawn from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
independently and identically,
and is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
, i.e.,
(\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})_{\tau\in[E]}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\mathcal{F}_{0}
.
The filtration is generated sequentially such that
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}=\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau-1})
,
and
\mathbf{w}_{\tau}\in\Theta
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau-1}
-measurable.
Then for a measurable function
\mathbf{h}^{\prime}:\Theta\times\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
assume that for any deterministic
\mathbf{w}\in\Theta
,

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\zeta}\sim\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w},\bm{\zeta})]=\vec{0}

and
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\zeta}\sim\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w},\bm{\zeta})\|^{2})]\leq 2
.
Then
\mathbf{z}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{E}}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{\tau},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})
,
satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]=\vec{0}
and a subgaussian bound:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]\leq 2

Proof for
Lemma

B.7
.

We show these elementary inqualities
(\exp(|u|/\sqrt{2})-1)^{2}\geq\exp|u|-|u|-1\geq\exp(u)-u-1
hold for any
u\in\mathbb{R}
, since

(\exp(|u|/\sqrt{2})-1)^{2}-(\exp|u|-|u|-1)=\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{(2^{k/2}+1)(2^{k/2}-2)}{2^{k/2}k!}|u|^{k}\geq 0

\exp|u|-|u|-1=\exp(u)-u-1+2(\sinh|u|-|u|)\mathds{1}_{u<0}\geq\exp(u)-u-1

Then by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have:

\displaystyle(\exp(\lambda^{2}/2)-1)(\exp(X^{2})-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{[\lambda/\sqrt{2}]^{2k}}{k!}\right)\left(\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{X^{2k}}{k!}\right)\geq\left(\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{(|\lambda X|/\sqrt{2})^{k}}{k!}\right)^{2}=\left(\exp(|\lambda X|/\sqrt{2})-1\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp(\lambda X)-\lambda X-1

By taking the expectation on both sides, and using
\mathbb{E}[X]=0
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(X^{2})]\leq 2
, we have:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda X)]\leq(\exp(\lambda^{2}/2)-1)(\mathbb{E}[\exp(X^{2})]-1)+\lambda\mathbb{E}[X]+1\leq\exp(\lambda^{2}/2)

∎

Proof for
Lemma

B.8
.

We introduce the partial sum of random vectors
\mathbf{S}_{\tau}=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\mathbf{z}_{\tau^{\prime}}
for all
\tau\in[E+1]
,
which is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable,
then
\mathbf{S}_{0}=\vec{0}
and
\mathbf{S}_{E}=\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{\tau}
.
By using
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq 2
and applying
Lemma

B.7
,
for
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable
\mathbf{S}_{\tau}
and any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\langle\mathbf{S}_{\tau},\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\rangle)\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2}}{2}\right)

Consider such a quantity

\exp(\lambda_{\tau}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2})
with a sequence
(\lambda_{\tau})_{\tau=1}^{E}
of positive numbers,
we will select
\lambda_{\tau}
wisely to establish a recursive relationship
between
\exp(\lambda_{\tau}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2})
and
\exp(\lambda_{\tau+1}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau+1}\|^{2})
.

From the defintion of
\mathbf{S}_{\tau}
and using
\mathbf{S}_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable, we have:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda_{\tau+1}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau+1}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq\exp(\lambda_{\tau+1}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda_{\tau+1}\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2}+2\lambda_{\tau+1}\langle\mathbf{S}_{\tau},\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\rangle)\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]

Then applying Hölder’s inequality
\mathbb{E}[|XY|\mid\mathcal{F}]\leq\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}\mid\mathcal{F}]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{E}[|Y|^{q}\mid\mathcal{F}]^{\frac{1}{q}}
with
p=1/\lambda_{\tau+1}
and
q=1/(1-\lambda_{\tau+1})
,
to upper bound the conditional expectation by relating it to

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2})]\leq 2
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\langle\mathbf{S}_{\tau},\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\rangle)\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq\exp(\frac{\lambda^{2}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2}}{2})
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda_{\tau+1}\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2}+2\lambda_{\tau+1}\langle\mathbf{S}_{\tau},\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\rangle)\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]^{\lambda_{\tau+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{2\lambda_{\tau+1}}{1-\lambda_{\tau+1}}\langle\mathbf{S}_{\tau},\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\rangle\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}\right]^{1-\lambda_{\tau+1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{\lambda_{\tau+1}}\exp\left((1-\lambda_{\tau+1})\cdot\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2\lambda_{\tau+1}}{1-\lambda_{\tau+1}}\right)^{2}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2}\right)

By selecting the sequence
(\lambda_{\tau})_{\tau=1}^{E}
such that
the final value
\lambda_{E}=\frac{1}{4E}
and satisfies the recursive relationship:

\lambda_{\tau}:=\lambda_{\tau+1}+(1-\lambda_{\tau+1})\cdot\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2\lambda_{\tau+1}}{1-\lambda_{\tau+1}}\right)^{2}=\lambda_{\tau+1}\cdot\frac{1+\lambda_{\tau+1}}{1-\lambda_{\tau+1}}

then we can establish the recursive relationship for
M_{\tau}:=\exp(\lambda_{\tau}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2}-\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau}\lambda_{\tau^{\prime}})

with
M_{0}=\exp(0)=1
:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda_{\tau+1}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau+1}\|^{2}-\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau+1}\lambda_{\tau^{\prime}})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq\exp(\lambda_{\tau}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2})\cdot 2^{\lambda_{\tau+1}}\exp(-\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau+1}\lambda_{\tau^{\prime}})\leq\exp(\lambda_{\tau}\|\mathbf{S}_{\tau}\|^{2}-\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau}\lambda_{\tau^{\prime}})

From the above recursive relationship, we show that
M_{\tau}
is a supermartingale,
namely
\mathbb{E}[M_{\tau+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq M_{\tau}
.
Then by the tower property of conditional expectation (Theorem 4.2.4 on page 189 of
Durrett 2019
) and the fact that
M_{0}=1
, we have:

\mathbb{E}[M_{E}\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]\leq\mathbb{E}[M_{0}\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]=M_{0}=1,\quad\lambda_{E}=\frac{1}{4E}\implies\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{1}{4E}\|\mathbf{S}_{E}\|^{2}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}\right]\leq\exp\left(\sum_{\tau=1}^{E}\lambda_{\tau}\right)

To upperbound
\sum_{\tau=1}^{E}\lambda_{\tau}
, we introduce
a_{\tau}:=\lambda_{E+1-\tau}
with initial value
a_{1}=\lambda_{E}=\frac{1}{4E}

and use the recursive relation:

\frac{1}{a_{\tau}}=\frac{1}{a_{1}}+\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau-1}\left(\frac{1}{a_{\tau^{\prime}+1}}-\frac{1}{a_{\tau^{\prime}}}\right)=4E-\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau-1}\frac{2}{1+a_{\tau^{\prime}}}>4E-2(\tau-1)

Therefore, we have:

\sum_{\tau=1}^{E}\lambda_{\tau}=\sum_{\tau=1}^{E}a_{\tau}<\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau=1}^{E}\frac{1}{E+(E+1-\tau)}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\tau=1}^{E}\frac{1}{E+\tau}<\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{E}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\tau+E}=\frac{\ln 2}{2}

By selecting
\mathbf{z}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{E}}\mathbf{S}_{E}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{E}}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{\tau}
, we have:

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{1}{4E}\|\mathbf{S}_{E}\|^{2}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}\right]\leq\exp\left(\frac{\ln 2}{2}\right)=\sqrt{2}<2

∎

B.9
Freezing Lemma: Conditional Expectation with Independent Random Variable.

Let
X
and
Z
be random variables on a probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
. Assume:

X
is
\mathcal{G}
-measurable, where
\mathcal{G}
is a sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathcal{F}
.

Z
is independent of
\mathcal{G}
. For any measurable function
h
satisfying
\mathbb{E}[|h(X,Z)|]<\infty
, then it holds almost surely that for fixed value
x
:

\mathbb{E}[h(X,Z)\mid\mathcal{G}]=\mathbb{E}[h(x,Z)]_{x=X}

.

This lemma is known as the “Freezing Lemma”,
and can be extended from random variables
X
and
Z
to random vectors
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{z}

with a measurable function
h
satisfies
\mathbb{E}[|h(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z})|]<\infty
such that

\mathbb{E}[h(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z})\mid\mathcal{G}]=\mathbb{E}[h(\cdot,\mathbf{z})]_{\cdot=\mathbf{x}}

Proof of
Lemma

B.9
.

We first establish the theorem for the case of indicator function, from which the general result follows by using the
monotone class argument
(see example of “Standard Machine” procedure in proof of Theorem 4.1.14 on page 184 of
Durrett 2019
).
Let’s begin with
h(x,z)=\mathds{1}_{A}(x)\cdot\mathds{1}_{C}(z)
, where
A
and
C
are Borel sets on the real line. Then
\mathbb{E}[h(x,Z)]_{x=X}=\mathds{1}_{A}(X)\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{C}(Z)]
is
\mathcal{G}
-measurable, since
X
is
\mathcal{G}
-measurable and
\mathds{1}_{A}(X)
is also
\mathcal{G}
-measurable, therefore it is
\mathcal{G}
-measurable as a constant multiple of a
\mathcal{G}
-measurable variable.
For any set of outcomes
B\in\mathcal{G}
, suppose that
\mathbb{E}[h(X,Z)\cdot\mathds{1}_{B}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[h(x,Z)]_{x=X}\cdot\mathds{1}_{B}]
holds, then it implies
\mathbb{E}[h(X,Z)\mid\mathcal{G}]=\mathbb{E}[h(x,Z)]_{x=X}
by the definition of conditional expectation on page 178 of
Durrett 2019
.
The two sides are identical for any
B\in\mathcal{G}
as shown below by using that
Z
is independent of
\mathcal{G}
and
X
is
\mathcal{G}
-measurable (see also pages 38-39 and page 184 of
Durrett 2019
).

\mathbb{E}[h(X,Z)\cdot\mathds{1}_{B}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{A}(X)\cdot\mathds{1}_{C}(Z)\cdot\mathds{1}_{B}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{A}(X)\cdot\mathds{1}_{B}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{C}(Z)]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[h(x,Z)]_{x=X}\cdot\mathds{1}_{B}]

Using
monotone class argument
, the above result is extended to all general measurable functions
h
with
\mathbb{E}[|h(X,Z)|]<\infty
. This monotone class argument involves three steps:
(1) the linearity of conditional expectation generalizes the property from indicator functions to all simple functions (finite linear combinations of indicators);
(2) the Monotone Convergence Theorem (Theorem 1.5.7 on page 23 of
Durrett 2019
) then extends it to all non-negative measurable functions;
(3) the decomposition of measurable function into its positive and negative parts (
h=h^{+}-h^{-}
) covers all measurable functions satisfying
\mathbb{E}[|h(X,Z)|]<\infty
.
∎

Proof of Remark 1 for
Lemma

B.8
.

As
\mathbf{w}_{0}\in\Theta
is
\mathcal{F}_{0}
-measurable, and
\mathcal{F}_{s}=\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{s-1},\zeta_{s})
, then we have
\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{s}
and therefore
\mathbf{w}_{0}
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable for all
s=1,2,\cdots,B
.
As
\mathcal{F}_{s}=\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{s-1},\zeta_{s})
, then
\zeta_{s}
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable,
and therfore
\mathbf{z}_{s}:=\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w}_{0},\zeta_{s})
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable.
Since
\zeta_{s}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
and all prior
\zeta_{1},...,\zeta_{s-1}
,
then
\zeta_{s}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{s-1}=\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{0},\zeta_{1},...,\zeta_{s-1})
.
Therefore, by applying the Freezing Lemma
Lemma

B.9
, we have:

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{s}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w}_{0},\zeta_{s})\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w},\zeta_{s})]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{0}}=\mathbb{E}_{\zeta\sim\mathcal{D}}[\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w},\zeta)]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{0}}=\vec{0}

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{s}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]=\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w}_{0},\zeta_{s})\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]=\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w},\zeta_{s})\|^{2})]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{0}}=\mathbb{E}_{\zeta\sim\mathcal{D}}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w},\zeta)\|^{2})]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{0}}\leq 2

Given
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{s}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]=\vec{0}
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{s}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]\leq 2
,
then by applying the subgaussian tail bound
Lemma

B.8

to
\mathbf{z}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{B}}\sum_{s=1}^{B}\mathbf{z}_{s}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{B}}\sum_{s=1}^{B}\mathbf{h}(\mathbf{w}_{0},\zeta_{s})
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]=\vec{0}
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]\leq 2
.
∎

Proof of Remark 2 for
Lemma

B.8
.

Since
\mathbf{w}_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable,
and
\bm{\zeta}_{\tau}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau+1}
-measurable since
\mathcal{F}_{\tau+1}=\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{\tau},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})
.
Therefore,
\mathbf{z}_{\tau}:=\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{\tau},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})
is
\mathcal{F}_{\tau+1}
-measurable.
Since
\bm{\zeta}_{\tau}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{0}
and all prior
\bm{\zeta}_{0},...,\bm{\zeta}_{\tau-1}
,
then
\bm{\zeta}_{\tau}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}=\sigma(\mathcal{F}_{0},\bm{\zeta}_{0},...,\bm{\zeta}_{\tau-1})
.
Therefore, by applying the Freezing Lemma
Lemma

B.9
, we have:

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{\tau},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{\tau}}=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\zeta}\sim\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w},\bm{\zeta})]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{\tau}}=\vec{0}

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{\tau},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})\|^{2})]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{\tau}}=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\zeta}\sim\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}[\exp(\|\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w},\bm{\zeta})\|^{2})]_{\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{\tau}}\leq 2

Given
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=\vec{0}
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{\tau}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]\leq 2
,
then by applying the subgaussian tail bound
Lemma

B.8

to
\mathbf{z}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{E}}\sum_{\tau=0}^{E-1}\mathbf{z}_{\tau}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{E}}\sum_{\tau=0}^{E-1}\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(\mathbf{w}_{\tau},\bm{\zeta}_{\tau})
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]=\vec{0}
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{0}]\leq 2
.
∎

B.10
Maximal Inequality for Conditionally Sub-Gaussian Martingale Differences.

Let a sequence of random vectors
(\mathbf{z}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}

and a sequence of indicator random variables
(\mathds{1}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

with
\mathds{1}_{k}\in\{0,1\}
be adapted to a filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{k+1})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
with
\mathcal{F}_{0}=\{\emptyset,\Omega\}
,
namely
\mathbf{z}_{k},\mathds{1}_{k}
are
\mathcal{F}_{k+1}
-measurable.
Assume that the sequence satisfies the martingale difference property

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]=\vec{0}
and
possesses a sub-Gaussian tail bound such that for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and any
i\in\mathcal{I}\equiv\{1,\cdots,n\}
:

\ln\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(\lambda[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}.

Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\sqrt{2\ln\frac{2Kn}{\delta}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}.

Proof.

The above inequality holds if
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0
, otherwise, we have
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}>0
.
Noting that the maximum is not less than the average of
\left\{\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}\mid\mathds{1}_{k}=1,k\in[K]\right\}
,
and the maximum whose index is in the subset
\{k\mid\mathds{1}_{k}=1,k\in[K]\}
is less than the maximum of the entire trajectory
[K]
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}\Bigg/\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\max_{k:\mathds{1}_{k}=1,k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}=\max_{k\in[K]}\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}|

The probability of
\max_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}>t
for some
t\geq 0
is bounded
by the probability of all
|[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}|>t
for any
i\in\mathcal{I}
and
k\in[K]
,
then taking the union bound over all
i\in\mathcal{I}
and
k\in[K]
.

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}|>t\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(|[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}|>t,\forall i\in\mathcal{I},k\in[K]\right)\leq\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{P}\left(|[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}|>t\right)

For any
t>0
, by the Law of Total Expectation and the conditional Chernoff bound
using the sub-Gaussian tail bound
\ln\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(\lambda[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}\nu}{2}
:

\mathbb{P}([\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}>t)=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}([\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}>t\mid\mathcal{F}_{k})]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\inf_{\lambda>0}\exp\left(-\lambda t+\ln\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(\lambda[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\right]\right)\right]\leq\exp\left(\inf_{\lambda>0}-\lambda t+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\right).

Similary, for any
t>0
,
we have
\mathbb{P}([\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}<-t)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\right)
, and
therefore, the probability of
|[\mathbf{z}_{k}]_{i}|>t
is bounded by
2\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{2}\right)
.
Setting the failure probability
2Kn\exp(-t^{2}/2)=\delta
and solving for
t
yields the high-probability threshold
t=\sqrt{2\ln(2Kn/\delta)}
.
Since the ratio is bounded by
\max_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}
, it is bounded by
t
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}}{\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}}>\sqrt{2\ln\frac{2Kn}{\delta}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}>\sqrt{2\ln\frac{2Kn}{\delta}}\right)\leq 2Kn\exp\left(-\frac{2\ln\frac{2Kn}{\delta}}{2}\right)=\delta.

∎

B.11
Upper Bound for Binomial Coefficient.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
n^{\prime},m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that
n\geq n^{\prime}\geq m
, then the following inequality holds:

\frac{\binom{n^{\prime}}{m}}{\binom{n}{m}}\leq\left(1-\frac{m}{n}\right)^{n\left(1-\frac{n^{\prime}}{n}\right)}

Proof.

Rewriting the ratio of two binomial coefficients as a product, applying the elementary inequality
1+t\leq\exp(t)
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
and lowerbounding a summation with a integral such that
\sum_{m^{\prime}\in[m]}\frac{1}{n-m^{\prime}}\geq\int_{0}^{m}\frac{1}{n-m^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}m^{\prime}=-\ln(1-\frac{m}{n})
.

\frac{\binom{n^{\prime}}{m}}{\binom{n}{m}}=\prod_{m^{\prime}\in[m]}\left(1-\frac{n-n^{\prime}}{n-m^{\prime}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-(n-n^{\prime})\sum_{m^{\prime}\in[m]}\frac{1}{n-m^{\prime}}\right)\leq\left(1-\frac{m}{n}\right)^{n\left(1-\frac{n^{\prime}}{n}\right)}

∎

B.12
Conditional Exponential Tail Bound for Maximum of a Subset with a Uniform CDF Bound.

Let
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, which satisfies the following condition (Uniform CDF Bound) with some
\sigma>0
and any
t\in\mathbb{R}
:

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathds{1}\{\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i^{\prime}}-[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\}\leq\left(1-\frac{t}{\sigma}\right)_{+}

A subset
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
with fixed cardinality
|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}|=m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
is selected from
\mathcal{I}
uniformly at random, which is independent of
\mathbf{x}
, namely
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\sim\text{Unif}(\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I}))
with
\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I}):=\{A\subseteq\mathcal{I}:|A|=m\}
, and

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=A)=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=A\mid\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})|}=\frac{1}{\binom{n}{m}},\quad\forall A\in\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})

Then the difference between the maximum
\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}
over the entire set
\mathcal{I}
, and the maximum
\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}
over the subset
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, is bounded as follows
when
r:=\frac{m}{n}\neq 1
:

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\mid\mathbf{x}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-r)]n}{\sigma}t\right)

\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\mid\mathbf{x}\right]\leq\frac{\sigma}{[-\ln(1-r)]n}<\frac{\sigma}{rn}=\frac{\sigma}{m}

Otherwise, if
r:=\frac{m}{n}=1
, then
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
and
\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}
.

Proof.

Let’s define the following set
\mathcal{I}_{t}
for some
t\geq 0
, then the condition can be rewritten as:

\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x}):=\{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\mid\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i^{\prime}}-[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\},\qquad\frac{|\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})|}{n}\leq\left(1-\frac{t}{\sigma}\right)_{+}

Noting that the event
\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t
is equivalent to

\max_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i^{\prime}}-[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t,\forall i\in\mathcal{I}
, namely
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})
.
Using
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{I}=A\mid\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})|}
,

\mathbb{P}(A\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})\mid\mathcal{I}=A;\mathbf{x})=\mathds{1}\{A\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})\}
,
letting
\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})):=\{A\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})\mid|A|=m\}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\mid\mathbf{x}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})\mid\mathbf{x}\right)=\sum_{A\in\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})}\mathbb{P}(A\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})\mid\mathcal{I}=A;\mathbf{x})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{I}=A\mid\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{A\in\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})}\mathds{1}\{A\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})\}}{|\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})|}=\frac{|\{A\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})\mid|A|=m\}|}{|\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})|}=\frac{|\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x}))|}{|\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})|}=\frac{\binom{|\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})|}{m}}{\binom{n}{m}}\mathds{1}_{m\leq|\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})|}

Noting
|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}|=m
, applying
Lemma

B.11
to upperbound the ratio of two binomial coefficients
by substituting
n^{\prime}\leftarrow|\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})|
,
and using the condition such that
\frac{|\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})|}{n}\leq\left(1-\frac{t}{\sigma}\right)_{+}

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\mid\mathbf{x}\right)=\frac{\binom{|\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})|}{|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}|}}{\binom{|\mathcal{I}|}{|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}|}}\mathds{1}_{|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}|\leq|\mathcal{I}_{t}(\mathbf{x})|}\leq\left(1-\frac{|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}|}{|\mathcal{I}|}\right)^{n\left[1-\left(1-\frac{t}{\sigma}\right)_{+}\right]}\mathds{1}\left\{\frac{|\mathcal{I}^{\prime}|}{|\mathcal{I}|}\leq\left(1-\frac{t}{\sigma}\right)_{+}\right\}

Therefore, by letting
r:=\frac{m}{n}
, we have
\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\mid\mathbf{x}\right)=0
when
t\geq\sigma
.
For any
t\geq 0
, we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\mid\mathbf{x}\right)\leq(1-r)^{n\frac{t}{\sigma}}\mathds{1}\{t\leq(1-r)\sigma\}

When
r:=\frac{m}{n}=1
, then
\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\mid\mathbf{x}\right)=\mathds{1}\{t=0\}
for
t\geq 0
, and
\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}
.
If
r:=\frac{m}{n}\neq 1
, then the upperbound of expectation is established as follows.

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\mid\mathbf{x}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-r)]n}{\sigma}t\right)

\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\mid\mathbf{x}\right]=\int_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}[\mathbf{x}]_{i}\geq t\right)\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{\sigma}{[-\ln(1-r)]n}<\frac{\sigma}{rn}=\frac{\sigma}{m}

∎

B.13
Conditional Exponential Tail Bound with Assumption of Uniformly Bounded Relative Gap.

Let a filtration
(\mathcal{G}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
be defined with
\mathcal{G}_{0}=\{\emptyset,\Omega\}
and

\mathcal{G}_{k}=\sigma(\mathcal{G}_{k-1},\mathcal{I}_{k-1},\mathcal{B}_{k-1})
,
where a sequence of subsets
(\mathcal{I}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\text{Unif}(\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I}))

with
\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})=\{A\subseteq\mathcal{I}\mid|A|=m\}
for some
0<m\leq n
and
\mathcal{I}\equiv\{1,\cdots,n\}
,
are independently and identically sampled from
\mathcal{C}_{m}(\mathcal{I})
, and are independent of
the collection of random sample batches
\mathcal{B}_{k}
at any time
k
.
Let
\mathbf{w}_{k}\in\Theta
be
\mathcal{G}_{k}
-measurable,
and
\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}:\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
be two measurable functions,
and assume that
4.9
of Uniformly Bounded Relative Gap holds for

\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}),\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w})
and their maximum values
F(\mathbf{w})\equiv\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w})]_{i},G(\mathbf{w})\equiv\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w})]_{i}
.
Then for
F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\equiv\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})]_{i}
and
G(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\equiv\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})]_{i}
,
there exist exponential tail bounds for the conditional probabilities of
F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})
and
G(\mathbf{w}_{k})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})
as follows for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-m/n)]n}{\sigma}t\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(G(\mathbf{w}_{k})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-m/n)]n}{\sigma}t\right).

Proof.

Applying
4.9
, for any fixed value
\mathbf{v}\in\Theta
,
t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

and subsets
\mathcal{I}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{I}

and substituting fixed values
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{v})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathcal{I}_{k}
in
Lemma

B.12
, respectively.

\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{F(\mathbf{v})-F(\mathbf{v};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}]=\mathbb{P}(F(\mathbf{v})-F(\mathbf{v};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-m/n)]n}{\sigma}t\right)

\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{G(\mathbf{v})-G(\mathbf{v};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}]=\mathbb{P}(G(\mathbf{v})-G(\mathbf{v};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-m/n)]n}{\sigma}t\right)

\mathcal{I}_{k}
is independent of
\mathcal{G}_{k}=\sigma(\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{B}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{I}_{k-1},\mathcal{B}_{k-1})
,
since
\mathcal{I}_{k}
is indepedndent of prior
\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{B}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{I}_{k-1},\mathcal{B}_{k-1}
.
Since
\mathbf{w}_{k}
is
\mathcal{G}_{k}
-measurable, then we show the following identities
by substituting fixed value
x\leftarrow\mathbf{v}
,
\mathcal{G}\leftarrow\mathcal{G}_{k},X\leftarrow\mathbf{w}_{k}
,
Z\leftarrow\mathcal{I}_{k};h(X,Z)\leftarrow\mathds{1}\{F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\},\mathds{1}\{G(\mathbf{w}_{k})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}
in
Lemma

B.9
, respectively.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{F(\mathbf{v})-F(\mathbf{v};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}]_{\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{w}_{k}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{G(\mathbf{w}_{k})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{G(\mathbf{v})-G(\mathbf{v};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}]_{\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{w}_{k}}

Combining the above identities, and the exponential bounds for any fixed value
\mathbf{v}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-m/n)]n}{\sigma}t\right),

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{P}\left(G(\mathbf{w}_{k})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}\{G(\mathbf{w}_{k})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})\geq t\}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{[-\ln(1-m/n)]n}{\sigma}t\right).

∎

B.14
Upper Bound for Sum of Random Variables with Conditional Exponential Tail.

Let nonnegative random variables
(Y_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

and a sequence of indicator random variables
(\mathds{1}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
be adapted to a fitlaration

(\mathcal{G}_{k+1})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}}
with
\mathcal{G}_{0}=\{\emptyset,\Omega\}
,
namely
Y_{k},\mathds{1}_{k}
are
\mathcal{G}_{k+1}
-measurable, and have the following one sided conditional tail bound with some
C>0

for any
t\geq 0
and any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
:

\mathbb{P}(Y_{k}\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k})\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t}{C}\right)

Then for
[K]\equiv\{0,1,2,\ldots,K-1\}
, with probability at least
1-\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Y_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq C\ln\frac{K}{\delta}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

For
\mathcal{S}\equiv\{k\in[K]\mid\mathds{1}_{k}=1\}
,
hence
|\mathcal{S}|=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}
, with a ratio
\kappa:=|\mathcal{S}|/K\in[0,1]

and such a convention of
\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\cdot\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=1,\ln\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\cdot\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0

when
|\mathcal{S}|=0
,
the following inequalities hold:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Y_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2C\ln\frac{2/\kappa}{\delta}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k},\qquad\sum_{k\in[K]}Y_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2C\left(\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\ln\frac{16}{\kappa}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\right)

Remark for
Lemma

B.14
.

By using the 2nd inequality and distributing the constant in logarithm, we have:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Y_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2C\ln\frac{4}{\delta}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}+2C\ln\frac{1}{2\kappa}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

By using the 3rd inequality, we have the following bound (see its derivation after the proof of the 3rd inequality):

\sum_{k\in[K]}Y_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2C\ln\frac{4}{\delta}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}+2C\ln\frac{4}{\delta}\cdot K

Proof of
Lemma

B.14
.

By letting
Z_{k}:=Y_{k}/C
, we have
\mathbb{P}(Z_{k}\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k-1})\leq\exp(-t)
for any
t\geq 0
and any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
We give the proofs for these three inequalities separately for
Z_{k}
, then we have these inequalities for
Y_{k}=CZ_{k}
automatically.

Proof of 1st inequality (using maximal inequality and union bound)

The first inequliaty holds trivially if
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0
, otherwise, we have
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}>0
,
then we have the following maximal inequality for nonnegative random variables
Z_{k}
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\Bigg/\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\max_{k\in[K],\mathds{1}_{k}=1}Z_{k}\leq\max_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}

By using
\mathbb{P}(Z_{k}\geq t)=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{Z_{k}\geq t}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}(Z_{k}\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k})]\leq\exp(-t),\forall t\geq 0
,
for some fixed
t=\ln\frac{K}{\delta}\geq 0
, the probability of
\max_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}\geq t
is bounded by
\delta

by taking the union bound over all
k\in[K]
.

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}>t\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}\geq t\right)=\mathbb{P}(Z_{k}\geq t,\forall k\in[K])\leq\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathbb{P}(Z_{k}\geq t)=K\exp(-t)=\delta

By substituting
t=\ln\frac{K}{\delta}
, we complete the proof of the first inequality for
Z_{k}
with probability at least
1-\delta

Proof of 2nd inequality (using AM-GM inequality and Markov’s inequality)

The second inequality holds trivially if
|\mathcal{S}|=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0
under the convention of
|\mathcal{S}|\cdot\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=1,\ln\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\cdot\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0
,
otherwise, we have
|\mathcal{S}|=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}>0
, then by applying the AM-GM inequality for some
\lambda>0
, we have:

\exp\left(\frac{\lambda}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\right)\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{k\in[K]}\exp(\lambda Z_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{k\in[K]}\exp(\lambda Z_{k})

Noting that for any nonnegative random variable
X
,

\mathbb{E}[X\mid\mathcal{F}]=\int_{0}^{1}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(X>u\mid\mathcal{F})\mathrm{d}u
.
Since
Z_{k}=Y_{k}/C
is nonnegative, we have
\mathbb{P}(\exp(\lambda Z_{k})>u\mid\mathcal{G}_{k-1})=1
for any
u\in[0,1],\lambda>0
,
and letting
u=\exp(\lambda t)
in the second integral and using the fact that
\mathbb{P}(Z_{k}\geq t\mid\mathcal{G}_{k-1})\leq\exp(-t)
,
we have the following inequality for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\lambda\in(0,1)
:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda Z_{k})\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]=\int_{0}^{1}1\mathrm{d}u+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\exp(\lambda Z_{k})>\exp(\lambda t)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k})\mathrm{d}(\exp(\lambda t))\leq 1+\lambda\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-[1-\lambda]t)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{1-\lambda}

By tower property of conditional expectation,
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda Z_{k})]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda Z_{k})\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]]\leq\frac{1}{1-\lambda}
,
then by applying Markov’s inequality for
\delta\in(0,1),t:=\frac{K}{(1-\lambda)\delta}
, we have the following bound:

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}\exp(\lambda Z_{k})>t\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k\in[K]}\exp(\lambda Z_{k})\right]/t\leq\frac{K}{(1-\lambda)t}=\delta

Therefore, we have the following inequality with probability at least
1-\delta
by substituting
t:=\frac{K}{(1-\lambda)\delta},\kappa:=|\mathcal{S}|/K
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\frac{|\mathcal{S}|}{\lambda}\ln\left(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{k\in[K]}\exp(\lambda Z_{k})\right)\leq\frac{\ln(t/|\mathcal{S}|)}{\lambda}\cdot|\mathcal{S}|=\frac{\ln\frac{2/\kappa}{\delta}-\ln(2(1-\lambda))}{\lambda}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

By selecting
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}
, we complete the proof of the second inequality for
Z_{k}
with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof of 3rd inequality (using supermartingale and combinatorics)

The third inequality holds trivially if
|\mathcal{S}|=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0

under the convention of
|\mathcal{S}|\cdot\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=1,\ln\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\cdot\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0
,
otherwise, we have
|\mathcal{S}|=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}>0
,
then for non-empty outcomes
\mathcal{A}_{j}
of the random set
\mathcal{S}\subseteq[K]

such that
|\mathcal{S}|=j
for
j\in\{1,2,\ldots,K\}
,

\mathcal{A}_{j}:=\{A_{j}\subseteq[K]\mid A_{j}\neq\emptyset,|A_{j}|=j\},\qquad|\mathcal{A}_{j}|=\binom{K}{j}\leq\left(\frac{\mathrm{e}K}{j}\right)^{j}=\exp\left(j\ln\frac{\mathrm{e}}{j/K}\right)

For any deterministic set
A_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
, we introduce the corresponding
(M_{k}(A_{j}))_{k\in\{0,1,\dots,K\}}
with
M_{0}(A_{j})=\exp(0)=1,M(A_{j})\equiv M_{K}(A_{j})
and

M_{k}(A_{j}):=\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{k^{\prime}\in[k]}Z_{k^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{k^{\prime}\in A_{j}}+\ln(1-\lambda)\sum_{k^{\prime}\in[k]}\mathds{1}_{k^{\prime}\in A_{j}}\right)
,
we have
M_{k}(A_{j})
is a supermartingale because for any
k\in[K]
, (see Theorem 4.2.4 on page 189 of
Durrett 2019
):

\mathbb{E}[M_{k+1}(A_{j})\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]=\left[(1-\lambda)\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda Z_{k})\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\right]^{\mathds{1}_{k\in A_{j}}}\cdot M_{k}(A_{j})\leq M_{k}(A_{j})

By using the tower property of conditional expectation, we have the following inequality for any
k\in[K]
:

\mathbb{E}[M(A_{j})]=\mathbb{E}[M_{K}(A_{j})]\leq\ldots\leq\mathbb{E}[M_{k+1}(A_{j})]\leq\mathbb{E}[M_{k}(A_{j})]\leq\mathbb{E}[M_{0}(A_{j})]=M_{0}(A_{j})=1

By applying the Markov’s inequality for
M(A_{j})=\exp(\lambda\sum_{k\in A_{j}}Z_{k}+|A_{j}|\ln(1-\lambda))

with some
\delta_{j}\in(0,1),t_{j}:=\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{j}}
:

\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda\sum_{k\in A_{j}}Z_{k}+|A_{j}|\ln(1-\lambda)>t_{j}\right)\leq\exp(-t_{j})\mathbb{E}[M(A_{j})]\leq\exp(-t_{j})=\delta_{j},\quad\forall A_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}

Therefore, by selecting
\delta_{j}
such that
\delta/(j(j+1))=|\mathcal{A}_{j}|\delta_{j}=\binom{K}{j}\delta_{j},\forall j\in\{1,2,\ldots,K\}

and taking the union bound over all possible outcomes
A_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}
, then for any fixed
\lambda\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda\sum_{k\in\mathcal{S}}Z_{k}+|\mathcal{S}|\ln(1-\lambda)>t_{|\mathcal{S}|},|\mathcal{S}|=j\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{A_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}}\left\{\lambda\sum_{k\in A_{j}}Z_{k}+|A_{j}|\ln(1-\lambda)>t_{j}\right\}\cap\{\mathcal{S}=A_{j}\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{A_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{j}}\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda\sum_{k\in A_{j}}Z_{k}+|A_{j}|\ln(1-\lambda)>t_{j}\right)\leq|\mathcal{A}_{j}|\cdot\delta_{j}=\frac{\delta}{j(j+1)}=\delta\left(\frac{1}{j}-\frac{1}{j+1}\right)

Therefore, for any fixed
\lambda\in(0,1)
:

\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda\sum_{k\in\mathcal{S}}Z_{k}+|\mathcal{S}|\ln(1-\lambda)>t_{|\mathcal{S}|}\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda\sum_{k\in\mathcal{S}}Z_{k}+|\mathcal{S}|\ln(1-\lambda)>t_{|\mathcal{S}|},|\mathcal{S}|=j\right)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\delta\left(\frac{1}{j}-\frac{1}{j+1}\right)<\delta

By definitions of
\mathcal{S},\kappa\equiv\frac{|\mathcal{S}|}{K}
,
noting that
t_{|\mathcal{S}|}=\ln\frac{1}{\delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}}=\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\ln(|\mathcal{S}|(1+|\mathcal{S}|))+\ln|\mathcal{A}_{|\mathcal{S}|}|\leq\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+|\mathcal{S}|+|\mathcal{S}|\ln\frac{\mathrm{e}}{\kappa}
and

|\mathcal{S}|=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}
,
we have the following inequality with probability at least
1-\delta
for any fixed
\lambda\in(0,1)
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Z_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(t_{|\mathcal{S}|}+\ln\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\ln\frac{\mathrm{e}^{2}/(1-\lambda)}{\kappa}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\right)

By selecting
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}
,
then
\mathrm{e}^{2}/(1-\lambda)=2\mathrm{e}^{2}<16
, we complete the proof for
Z_{k}
with probability at least
1-\delta
.
∎

Proof of Remark for
Lemma

B.14
.

The latter bound in the remark holds trivially if
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=0
,
otherwise, we have
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}>0
, and
noting that
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}+K=(1+\kappa)K,\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}=\kappa K

from the definition of
\kappa
, the third inequality in
Lemma

B.14
becomes:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Y_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2C\left(\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\ln\frac{16}{\kappa}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\right)=2C\left(\frac{1}{(1+\kappa)K}\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{\kappa}{1+\kappa}\ln\frac{16}{\kappa}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}+K\right)

By using
\frac{1}{(1+\kappa)K}\leq 1
and
\frac{\kappa}{1+\kappa}\ln\frac{16}{\kappa}\leq\max_{\kappa\in[0,1]}\frac{\kappa}{1+\kappa}\ln\frac{16}{\kappa}=\ln 4
,
and noting
\ln\frac{1}{\delta}+\ln 4=\ln\frac{4}{\delta}
,
we establish the latter bound in the remark:

\sum_{k\in[K]}Y_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2C\ln\frac{4}{\delta}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}+2C\ln\frac{4}{\delta}\cdot K
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof for Result of Federated Learning with Full Participation

Proof for
4.7
of
Algorithm

3
.

By applying
Lemma

B.2
to the update rule
\mathbf{w}_{k+1}=\mathbf{w}_{k}-\eta\mathbf{u}_{k}
with step size
\eta>0
, we have

\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left(\|\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|^{2}-\|\mathbf{w}_{k+1}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|^{2}\right)+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}

For brevity, we write
\mathds{1}_{k}:=\mathds{1}_{G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}}

with
G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})=m(\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}),\alpha)
.
The local direction
\mathbf{u}_{k}
is given by the following equation
and local updates are given by
\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau+1}^{(i)}=\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}-\gamma\left[\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}+\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]\right]
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=\frac{\mathbf{w}_{k,0}^{(i)}-\mathbf{w}_{k,E}^{(i)}}{\gamma E}=\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

where
\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
is the sample at the
\tau
-th local update step of the
k
-th epoch for the
i
-th client,
and
\mathbf{w}_{k,0}^{(i)}=\mathbf{w}_{k}
is the initial local parameters for the
i
-th client.
The direction
\mathbf{u}_{k}
is given by the following equation with
the brevity notations
\mathbf{p}_{k}:=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}))
and
\mathbf{q}_{k}:=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}))
,
and we introduce
\mathbf{r}_{k}:=\mathbf{p}_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}+\mathbf{q}_{k}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{k}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}\mathds{1}_{k}+[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}])\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}

To help the analaysis, we introduce the following notations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}=\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}],\quad\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}=\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}+\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

Step 1: Upperbound
\eta\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}
and Lowerbound
\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

Then we can decompose the direction as
\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}+(\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)})=\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-(\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)})-(\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)})
.

1.1: upperbound of
\eta\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}

Then by using
\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{x}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{y}\|^{2},\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]

and the assumption of Lipschitz continuity
4.2
(
\|\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w})\|\leq L,\|\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w})\|\leq L
,
therefore
\|\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|\leq L,\left\|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\right\|\leq L

using
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}=1
and all
[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\geq 0
):

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}=\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\left\|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\leq\eta L^{2}K+\eta\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}

1.2: decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

We decompose the inner product by
introducing the Bregman divergence

D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\prime}||\mathbf{w}]:=f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\prime})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w})-\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}),\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\mathbf{w}\rangle\geq 0,D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\prime}||\mathbf{w}]:=g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\prime})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w})-\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}),\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\mathbf{w}\rangle\geq 0

for convex functions
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
from
4.1
,
therefore
\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=(f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})+D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}])\mathds{1}_{k}+(g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})+D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}])[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}(f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}(g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

1.3: lowerbound of 1st and 2nd terms in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

Regarding the first and second terms in the above equation for the inner product,
using the definition of
F=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}f_{i}
and
G=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}g_{i}
,
from the definition of
\mathbf{w}^{\ast}
such that
G(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq 0
in
Eq.

∗

.

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}),\quad\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq G(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq 0

Applying the deviation bound of softmax mean
Lemma

B.6

with
\alpha\geq\frac{2\ln n}{\epsilon^{\prime}}
and substituting
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k}),\bm{\delta}\leftarrow\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})

and
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k}),\bm{\delta}\leftarrow\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})
, then we have

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\geq F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-2\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]\geq\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2}-\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\right)[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

Combining the above, we have the following lower bound for the first and second terms in the equation for the inner product:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}(f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}(g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+\frac{\epsilon K}{2}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}+\epsilon}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

1.4: upperbound of 3rd and 4th terms in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

Regarding the third term and fourth term in the equation for the inner product,
we noting the three-point Bregman divergence identity
Lemma

B.1

by substituting
\psi\leftarrow f_{i},g_{i}
,
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow\mathbf{w}_{k}
,
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{w}^{\ast}
,
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\leftarrow\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
,
and using the fact that
D_{f_{i}}[\cdot||\cdot]\geq 0,D_{g_{i}}[\cdot||\cdot]\geq 0
from the definition of Bregman divergence
for convex functions
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
from
4.1
.

-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}]-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]\leq 0

-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}]-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]\leq 0

Using
(\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}=\gamma\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}

and
(\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]=\gamma\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
from the update rule,
and applying Abel’s summation formula
\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\langle\bm{a}_{\tau},\mathbf{B}_{\tau}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{A}_{E},\mathbf{B}_{E-1}\rangle-\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\langle\mathbf{A}_{\tau+1},\bm{b}_{\tau}\rangle

with
\bm{a}_{0}=\vec{0},\mathbf{A}_{\tau}:=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\bm{a}_{\tau^{\prime}},\mathbf{B}_{\tau}:=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\bm{b}_{\tau^{\prime}}

and substituting
\bm{a}_{\tau}\leftarrow\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\bm{b}_{\tau}\leftarrow\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})
, then
again applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lipschitz continuity
4.2
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\left\langle\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2L},\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{L}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\left\langle\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2L},\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle-4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau+1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{2L},\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}(E-1)\mathds{1}_{k}+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\|\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)},\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

where the intermediate vector
\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)}:=-\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau+1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{2L}

satisfies
\|\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)}\|\leq 1
by the assumption of Lipschitz continuity
4.2

and hence
\|\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\|\leq 2L
.
Similarly, by introducing the intermediate vector
\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)}:=-\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau+1]}\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{2L}

satisfies
\|\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)}\|\leq 1
by the assumption of Lipschitz continuity
4.2

and hence
\|\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\|\leq 2L
, then:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\rangle[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}(E-1)[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\|[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)},\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

We introduce the following notations,
\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}:=\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)}\mathds{1}_{k}+\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

and
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}:=\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
.
Summing over
\tau\in[E]
and
i\in\mathcal{I}

and noting
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}=1
and
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}=1
,
the upperbound of the 3rd and 4th terms in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle
is given by:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}(E-1)K+\frac{4\gamma L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|+\frac{4\gamma L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

1.5: rewriting the 5th term in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

We introduce the the direction vector
\mathbf{d}_{k}:=\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\ast}-\mathbf{w}_{k}}{D}

which satisfies
\|\mathbf{d}_{k}\|\leq 1
by the assumption of finite diameter
D

of the parameter space
\Theta

4.3
.
From the definition of
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}
, we have:

\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=-\frac{2\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}

Therefore, the 5th term in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

can be rewritten as:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=\frac{2D\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left\langle\mathbf{d}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

1.6: rewriting
\|\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}
in the upper bound of
\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}

By using the above equation with
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}
,
the term in the upper bound of
\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}
can be rewritten as:

\eta\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}=\frac{4\eta\sigma^{2}_{g}}{B_{g}}\cdot\frac{1}{E^{2}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

Step 2: Upperbound
\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}
and
\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}G(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}

We introduce a filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}}
to track the information up to time

t:=\text{ind}(k,i,\tau)\equiv k\cdot nE+i\cdot E+\tau
and

\mathcal{F}_{t}:=\sigma((\bm{\zeta}_{k^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}}^{(i^{\prime})})_{\text{ind}(k^{\prime},i^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})<t},(\bm{\xi}_{k^{\prime}})_{\text{ind}(k^{\prime},0,0)\leq t})
with

-measurable;

and is

)

and the assumption of Lipschitz continuity

)
and are

-measurable; (from the definition of

and
