)

\mathbf{d}_{k}\equiv\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\ast}-\mathbf{w}_{k}}{D},\quad\mathbf{e}_{k}^{(i)}\equiv\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})}{2L}\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}=1}^{\tau}\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})}{2L}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

4. the condtional 1-subgaussian random variable
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}

such that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]=0

and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\|\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2})\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq 2

and therefore

\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\langle\mathbf{e},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\rangle)\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq\ln\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\|\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\|)\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2},\forall\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
and is
\mathcal{F}_{t+1}
-measurable. (from the definition of
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
,
independence of all
\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
and
\bm{\xi}_{k}\equiv(\bm{\xi}_{k}^{(i)})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}

and the assumption of sub-Gaussianity of stochastic gradients
4.4
,
and
Lemma

B.7
for subgaussianity of random variables,

Lemma

B.8
for subgaussianity of the average of subgaussian random vectors)

\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\equiv\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

2.1: terms in the upperbound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}

By using the polarization identity
Lemma

B.2
and
the established lower bound of
\eta\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}
and
the lowerbound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle
in the previous step,
dropping the nonnegative term
\frac{\|\mathbf{w}_{K}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|^{2}}{2\eta}
,
noting the assumption of finite diameter of the parameter space
\Theta

4.3

then
\|\mathbf{w}_{0}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|\leq D
,
we rearrange the terms and obtain the following upperbound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}+\epsilon}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]-\frac{\epsilon K}{2}+\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta}+\eta L^{2}K+2\gamma L^{2}(E-1)K

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4\eta\sigma^{2}_{g}}{B_{g}}\cdot\frac{1}{E^{2}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}+\frac{4\gamma L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2D\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left\langle\mathbf{d}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle+\frac{4\gamma L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

By choosing the local step size
\gamma=\frac{\eta}{E}
,
and applying triangle inequality
\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|\leq\|\mathbf{x}\|+\|\mathbf{y}\|
, then:

\displaystyle\frac{4\gamma L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|\leq\frac{4\eta L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|+\frac{4\eta L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{z}_{k,E-1}^{(i)}\|

Noting
\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]
and
2\sqrt{xy}\leq x+y
for any
x,y\geq 0
, then we have:

\displaystyle\frac{4\eta L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|=\sum_{k\in[K]}2\sqrt{\eta L^{2}\times\frac{4\eta\sigma^{2}_{g}}{B_{g}}\cdot\frac{1}{E^{2}}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}2\sqrt{\eta L^{2}\times\frac{4\eta\sigma^{2}_{g}}{B_{g}}\cdot\frac{1}{E^{2}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}}\leq\eta L^{2}K+\frac{4\eta\sigma^{2}_{g}}{B_{g}}\cdot\frac{1}{E^{2}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

Hence, we can upperbound
\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}
as:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}+\epsilon}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]-\frac{\epsilon K}{2}+\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta}+4\eta L^{2}K\left(1-\frac{1}{2E}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+\frac{8\eta\sigma^{2}_{g}}{B_{g}}\cdot\frac{1}{E^{2}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2D\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left\langle\mathbf{d}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle+\frac{4\eta L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4\eta L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{z}_{k,E-1}^{(i)}\|

2.2: terms in the upperbound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}G(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}

Noting
G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}=G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})\mathds{1}_{G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathds{1}_{k}
,
applying
Lemma

B.4
of softmax mean
m(\mathbf{x},\alpha)
with softmax hyperparameter
\alpha\geq\frac{2\ln n}{\epsilon^{\prime}}

then
G(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})]_{i}-m(\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}),\alpha)\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}
,
using

G(\mathbf{w})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})]_{i}-\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})]_{i}\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})]_{i}\leq\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}G(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})+[G(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})]+[G(\mathbf{w}_{k})-G(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})]\right]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon+\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}

2.3: upperbounds of

\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}
and

\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k},[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

By applying the established
Lemmas

B.7
and
B.8

for subgaussianity of random variables and subgaussianity of the average of subgaussian random vectors
with
4.4
,
and
Lemma

B.10

of maximal inequality of subgaussian random variables
with
\mathbf{z}_{k}\leftarrow\frac{\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})}{2\sigma_{\zeta}/\sqrt{B_{\zeta}}},\frac{\mathbf{g}_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k})}{2\sigma_{\zeta}/\sqrt{B_{\zeta}}}
,
\mathds{1}_{k}\leftarrow\mathds{1}_{k},[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
and
\delta\leftarrow\frac{\delta}{8}
,
the following upperbounds hold with probability at least
1-3\times\frac{\delta}{6}=1-\frac{\delta}{2}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}\leq 2\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]\leq 2\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

2.4: upper bound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

For brevity,
with the correspondence
t:=\text{ind}(k,i,\tau)\equiv k\cdot nE+i\cdot E+\tau
,
we define such a filtration
(\mathcal{G}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}}
by

\mathcal{G}_{-1}=\{\emptyset,\Omega\}
and

\mathcal{G}_{k}=\mathcal{F}_{nE\lceil\frac{t}{nE}\rceil}
,
and
\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{E}}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
.
Then from the definition and properties of
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
listed
at the beginning of the Step 2, we have:

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]=\vec{0},\quad\ln[\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]]\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2},\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R},

and
\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}
is
\mathcal{G}_{k+1}
-measurable.
Morevover,
\mathbf{r}_{k}
is
\mathcal{G}_{k}
-measurable from definitions of
\mathbf{r}_{k}
and
\mathcal{G}_{k}
.
We rewrite the summation as:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}=4E\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}=4E\sum_{k\in[K]}X_{k}

where
X_{k}:=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}
.
Then using Jensen’s inequality with
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}=1,[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\geq 0

and
\mathbf{r}_{k}
is
\mathcal{G}_{k}
-measurable,
and the tower property of conditional expectation, we have the following inequality
when
\lambda\in(0,1)
:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda X_{k}/2)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}/2)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\leq\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}/2)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]\leq(1-\lambda)^{-1/2}

since by introducing an independent standard Gaussian
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
using
\mathbb{E}_{z}[\exp(\lambda z^{2}/2)]=(1-\lambda)^{-1/2}
for
\lambda\in(0,1)
:

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}/2)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}_{z}[\exp(\sqrt{\lambda}\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|z)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}]]=\mathbb{E}_{z}[\mathbb{E}[\exp(\sqrt{\lambda}z\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k},z]]\leq\mathbb{E}_{z}[\exp(\lambda z^{2}/2)]=(1-\lambda)^{-1/2}

Therefore, noting
X_{k}
is
\mathcal{G}_{k+1}
-measurable, for any
\lambda\in(0,1)
, we have:

-K\frac{\lambda}{2}+\ln\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}X_{k}\right)\right]=-\lambda\frac{K}{2}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\ln\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}X_{k}\right)\mid\mathcal{G}_{k}\right]\leq\frac{K}{2}\left[-\lambda-\ln(1-\lambda)\right]\leq\frac{K}{4}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(1-\lambda)}

By using the Chernoff bound, and introducing
\psi^{\ast}(u):=\sup_{\lambda\in(0,1)}u\lambda-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{(1-\lambda)}
, we have:

\ln\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}X_{k}\geq K(1+u/2)\right)\leq\inf_{\lambda>0}-\frac{K}{4}(2+u)\lambda+\ln\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}X_{k}\right)\right]\leq-\frac{K}{4}\psi^{\ast}(u)

By the characterization of sub-gamma random variables, see also equation (2.5) in section 2.4 sub-gamma random variables, on page 29 of
Boucheron et al. 2013
.
we have
\psi^{\ast}(u)=(\sqrt{1+u}-1)^{2}
with its inverse function

\psi^{\ast-1}(v)=2\sqrt{v}+v
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}X_{k}\geq K(1+\psi^{\ast-1}(v)/2)\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{k\in[K]}X_{k}\geq K(1+\sqrt{v}+v/2)\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{K}{4}v\right)

By selecting
v=\frac{4\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}

and noting
1+\sqrt{v}+v/2\leq\frac{3}{2}+v
, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{8}
, we have:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}=4E\sum_{k\in[K]}X_{k}\leq 4E\cdot K(1+\sqrt{v}+v/2)\leq 4E\cdot K\left(\frac{3}{2}+v\right)=2E\left(3K+8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}\right)

2.5: upper bound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left\langle\mathbf{d}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

With the correspondence
t:=\text{ind}(k,i,\tau)\equiv k\cdot nE+i\cdot E+\tau
,
we define such intermediate quantities,

T=K\cdot n\cdot E
,

\mathbf{v}_{t}:=[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbf{d}_{k}
,
\mathbf{z}_{t}:=\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
,
and
S_{t}:=\sum_{t^{\prime}\in[t]}\langle\mathbf{v}_{t^{\prime}},\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\rangle
,

V_{t}:=\sum_{t^{\prime}\in[t]}\|\mathbf{v}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{2}
.
Then, since
\mathbf{z}_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t+1}
-measurable
and
\mathbf{v}_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable, then
S_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable,

V_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable.

Then for
M_{t}:=\exp\left(\lambda S_{t}-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}V_{t}\right)
,
which is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable,
we show it is a supermartingale
by using Theorem 4.2.4 on page 189 of
Durrett 2019
ans showing
that the following inequality holds:

\mathbb{E}[M_{t+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]=M_{t}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{z}_{t}\rangle-\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\|\mathbf{v}_{t}\|^{2}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]\leq M_{t}

Therefore, we have
\mathbb{E}[M_{T}]\leq M_{0}=1
by the tower property of conditional expectation,
which implies:

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{t\in[T]}\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{z}_{t}\rangle\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in[T]}\|\mathbf{v}_{t}\|^{2}\right)\right]\leq\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}KE\right)

since we have the following fact using
\|\mathbf{d}_{k}\|\leq 1
and
\sum_{i\in[\mathcal{I}]}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}^{2}\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}=1
:

\sum_{t\in[T]}\|\mathbf{v}_{t}\|^{2}=\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}^{2}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\|\mathbf{d}_{k}\|^{2}\leq KE

By using the Chernoff bound, then for any
u>0
, we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{t\in[T]}\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{z}_{t}\rangle\geq u\right)\leq\exp\left(\inf_{\lambda>0}-\lambda u+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}KE\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{u^{2}}{2KE}\right)

By selecting
u=\sqrt{2KE\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}
, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{8}
, we have:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left\langle\mathbf{d}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle=\sum_{t\in[T]}\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{z}_{t}\rangle\leq\sqrt{2KE\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}

2.6: upper bound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

With the correspondence
t:=\text{ind}(k,i,\tau)\equiv k\cdot nE+i\cdot E+\tau
,
we define such intermediate quantities,

T=K\cdot n\cdot E
,

\mathbf{v}_{t}:=[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\tau\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\mathds{1}_{\tau\in[E-1]}
,
\mathbf{z}_{t}:=\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
,
then by the same procedure of showing supermartingale, we also have

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{t\in[T]}\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{z}_{t}\rangle\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in[T]}\|\mathbf{v}_{t}\|^{2}\right)\right]\leq\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\frac{K}{3}(E-1)(E-3/2)(E-2)\right)

since we have the following fact using
\|\mathbf{e}_{k}\|\leq 1,\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau^{2}=\frac{1}{3}(E-1)(E-3/2)(E-2)
and
\sum_{i\in[\mathcal{I}]}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}^{2}\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}=1
:

\sum_{t\in[T]}\|\mathbf{v}_{t}\|^{2}=\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}^{2}\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau^{2}\|\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\|^{2}\leq\frac{K}{3}(E-1)(E-3/2)(E-2)

By the same procudure of taking Chernoff bound,
with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{8}
, we have:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle=\sum_{t\in[T]}\langle\mathbf{v}_{t},\mathbf{z}_{t}\rangle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2K}{3}(E-1)(E-3/2)(E-2)\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}

2.7: upper bound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{z}_{k,E-1}^{(i)}\|

With the correspondence
t:=\text{ind}(k,i,\tau)\equiv k\cdot nE+i\cdot E+\tau
,
we define such intermediate quantities,

T=K\cdot n\cdot E
,

a_{t}:=[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathds{1}_{\tau=E-1}
,
z_{t}:=\|\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\|
,
then by the same procedure of showing supermartingale, we also have

\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\sum_{t\in[T]}a_{t}z_{t}\right)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in[T]}a_{t}^{2}\right)\right]\leq\exp\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{2}K\right)

Since
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}^{2}\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}=1
, we have:

\sum_{t\in[T]}a_{t}^{2}=\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}^{2}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathds{1}_{\tau=E-1}^{2}=\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}^{2}\leq K

By the same procedure of taking Chernoff bound,
with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{8}
, we have:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{z}_{k,E-1}^{(i)}\|=\sum_{t\in[T]}a_{t}z_{t}\leq\sqrt{2K\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}

Step 3. Establish Final Bounds

3.1: rearranging terms in final bounds

By selecting
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon-4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}
,
and rearranging the terms with the established bounds in Step 2, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]-\frac{\epsilon K}{2}+\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta}+4\eta L^{2}K\left(1-\frac{1}{2E}\right)

\displaystyle+2\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\cdot K+\frac{16\eta\sigma_{g}^{2}}{B_{g}E}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)\cdot K

\displaystyle+2D\sigma_{g}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{B_{g}KE}}\cdot K+4\eta L\sigma_{g}\sqrt{\frac{2E}{3B_{g}K}\left(1-\frac{1}{E}\right)\left(1-\frac{3}{2E}\right)\left(1-\frac{2}{E}\right)\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}\cdot K

\displaystyle+4\eta L\sigma_{g}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{B_{g}K}}\cdot K

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}G(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

with probability at least
1-\delta
with
step sizes
\eta,\gamma
such that
\gamma=\frac{\eta}{E}
, and
softmax hyperparameter
\alpha\geq\frac{2\ln n}{\epsilon^{\prime}}
.

3.2: selecting step sizes
\eta,\gamma
, tolerance
\epsilon
and softmax hyperparameter
\alpha

By balancing the terms
\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta},4\eta L^{2}K
, we selecting step sizes as:

\eta=\frac{D}{L\sqrt{8K}},\quad\gamma=\frac{D}{LE\sqrt{8K}}

By upper bounding
1-\frac{1}{2E}\leq 1,\sqrt{(1-\frac{1}{E})(1-\frac{3}{2E})(1-\frac{2}{E})}\leq 1-\frac{9/4}{E+2}
,
ans substituting the global step size
\eta
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]-\frac{\epsilon K}{2}+\frac{DL}{\sqrt{K/8}}\cdot K+2\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\cdot K

\displaystyle+\frac{D\sigma_{g}^{2}}{LBE\sqrt{K/32}}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)\cdot K+2D\sigma_{g}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{BKE}}\left(1+\frac{E}{\sqrt{6K}}\left(1-\frac{9/4}{E+2}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2KE}}\right)\cdot K

Noting that for
E=1
, we can show that the above bound still holds without
\frac{E}{\sqrt{6K}}(1-\frac{9/4}{E+2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2KE}}
in the last term
since terms with
\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}
are 0 when
E=1
. For
E\geq 2
,
we note that
\frac{E}{\sqrt{6K}}(1-\frac{9/4}{E+2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2KE}}\leq\frac{E}{\sqrt{6K}}
is valid.
Combining these two cases, and introducing the “effective” gradient variance
\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}:=\frac{\sigma_{g}^{2}/B_{g}}{L^{2}E}
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{K}{2}\left[-\epsilon+\frac{DL}{\sqrt{K/32}}\left[1+2\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)+\bar{\sigma}_{g}\sqrt{\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}\left(1+\frac{E}{\sqrt{6K}}\right)\right]+4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\right]

By letting the sum of constant terms to be 0, noting
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon-4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}
, we obtain the following tolerance:

\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{DL}{\sqrt{K/32}}\left[1+2\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)+\bar{\sigma}_{g}\sqrt{\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}\left(1+\frac{E}{\sqrt{6K}}\right)\right]

\epsilon=\frac{DL}{\sqrt{K/32}}\left[1+2\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)+\bar{\sigma}_{g}\sqrt{\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}\left(1+\frac{E}{\sqrt{6K}}\right)\right]+4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}

Then, we obtain the following inequality with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\right]\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

We show
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\neq 0
, otherwise, from the above inequlaity,
and using the definition of
\mathds{1}_{k}\equiv\mathds{1}_{G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}}
, we have

G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}>0,[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]=1,\mathds{1}_{k}=0
for all
k\in[K]
,
which leads to a contradiction as follows:

0<\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+0\leq\epsilon\cdot 0=0

Noting that
0\leq[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
,
we have the following inequality with
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\neq 0
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\right]\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

By introducing a probability measure
\mathbf{P}_{K}
on the set of iterations
[K]
such that
\mathbf{P}_{K}(k)=\mathds{1}_{k}/\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k},\forall k\in[K]
,
and using the convexity of
F,G
(
4.1
and
Lemma

B.3
) and defining
\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{K}:=\mathbb{E}_{k\sim\mathbf{P}_{K}}[\mathbf{w}_{k}]=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathbf{w}_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}/\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}
, then:

F(\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{K})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq\mathbb{E}_{k\sim\mathbf{P}_{K}}\left[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\right]\leq\epsilon

G(\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{K})\leq\mathbb{E}_{k\sim\mathbf{P}_{K}}[G(\mathbf{w}_{k})]\leq\epsilon

when the softmax hyperparameter
\alpha\geq\frac{2\ln n}{\epsilon^{\prime}}
is large enough.
∎

Appendix D
Proof for Result of Switching Strategy with Full Participation

Proof for
4.6
of
Algorithm

2
.

When the number of local updates
E=1
of full participation case
of
Algorithm

3
,
it becomes the simple version
Algorithm

2
.
The Step 1 and Step 2 in the proof for
Algorithm

2
are the same as the Step 1 and Step 2 in the proof of

Algorithm

3
,
except replacing
\frac{\delta}{8}
with
\frac{\delta}{4}

in steps 2.4-2.5 since the terms in 2.6-2.7 of
\sum_{\tau=[E-1]}
are 0 in the upper bound of
\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}
is 0
when
E=1
,
noting that in step 2.4
\|\mathbf{z}_{k}^{(i)}\|
is
\frac{1}{4}
-subgaussian when
E=1

(tighter than 1-subgaussian in general proof) therefore
\frac{1}{4}\times
“original upper bound”
is still a valid upper bound.

Step 3. Establish Final Bounds

3.1: rearranging terms in final bounds

By selecting
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon-4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B}}
,
and rearranging the terms with the established bounds in Step 2 and letting
E=1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]-\frac{\epsilon K}{2}+\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta}+2\eta L^{2}K

\displaystyle+2\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\cdot K+\frac{2\eta\sigma_{g}^{2}}{B_{g}}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)\cdot K+2D\sigma_{g}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{B_{g}K}}\cdot K

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}G(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

with probability at least
1-\delta
and
softmax hyperparameter
\alpha\geq\frac{2\ln n}{\epsilon^{\prime}}
.

3.2: selecting step sizes
\eta,\gamma
, tolerance
\epsilon
and softmax hyperparameter
\alpha

By balancing the terms
\frac{D^{2}}{2\eta},2\eta L^{2}K
, we selecting step sizes as:

\eta=\frac{D}{2L\sqrt{K}}
.
Substituting the global step size
\eta
,
and introducing the “effective” gradient variance
\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}:=\frac{\sigma_{g}^{2}/B_{g}}{L^{2}\times 1}
, we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle+\frac{K}{2}\left[-\epsilon+\frac{2DL}{\sqrt{K}}\left[1+\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)+\bar{\sigma}_{g}\sqrt{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}\right]+4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}\right]

By letting the sum of constant terms to be 0, we obtain the following tolerance:

\epsilon=\frac{2DL}{\sqrt{K}}\left[1+\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)+\bar{\sigma}_{g}\sqrt{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}\right]+4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}

\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon-4\sigma_{\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{2\ln\frac{12Kn}{\delta}}{B_{\zeta}}}=\frac{2DL}{\sqrt{K}}\left[1+\bar{\sigma}_{g}^{2}\left(3+\frac{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}{K}\right)+\bar{\sigma}_{g}\sqrt{8\ln\frac{8}{\delta}}\right]

Then, we obtain the following inequality with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\right]\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

We show
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\neq 0
, otherwise, from the above inequlaity,
and using the definition of
\mathds{1}_{k}\equiv\mathds{1}_{G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}}
, we have

G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}>0,[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]=1,\mathds{1}_{k}=0
for all
k\in[K]
,
which leads to a contradiction as follows:

0<\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+0\leq\epsilon\cdot 0=0

Noting that
0\leq[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
,
we have the following inequality with
\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}\neq 0
:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\right]\mathds{1}_{k}\leq\epsilon\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

By introducing a probability measure
\mathbf{P}_{K}
on the set of iterations
[K]
such that
\mathbf{P}_{K}(k)=\mathds{1}_{k}/\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k},\forall k\in[K]
,
and using the convexity of
F,G
(
4.1
and
Lemma

B.3
) and defining
\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{K}:=\mathbb{E}_{k\sim\mathbf{P}_{K}}[\mathbf{w}_{k}]=\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathbf{w}_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}/\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}
, then:

F(\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{K})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq\mathbb{E}_{k\sim\mathbf{P}_{K}}\left[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\right]\leq\epsilon

G(\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{K})\leq\mathbb{E}_{k\sim\mathbf{P}_{K}}[G(\mathbf{w}_{k})]\leq\epsilon

when the softmax hyperparameter
\alpha\geq\frac{2\ln n}{\epsilon^{\prime}}
is large enough.

∎

Appendix E
Proof for Result of Federated Learning with Partial Participation

Proof for
4.10
of
Algorithm

1
.

By applying
Lemma

B.2
to the update rule
\mathbf{w}_{k+1}=\mathbf{w}_{k}-\eta\mathbf{u}_{k}
with step size
\eta>0
, we have

\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=\frac{1}{2\eta}\left(\|\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|^{2}-\|\mathbf{w}_{k+1}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\|^{2}\right)+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}

For brevity, we write
\mathds{1}_{k}:=\mathds{1}_{G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}}

with
G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})=m(\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}),\alpha)
.
The local direction
\mathbf{u}_{k}
is given by the following equation
and local updates are given by
\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau+1}^{(i)}=\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}-\gamma\left[\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}+\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]\right]
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=\frac{\mathbf{w}_{k,0}^{(i)}-\mathbf{w}_{k,E}^{(i)}}{\gamma E}=\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

where
\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
is the sample at the
\tau
-th local update step of the
k
-th epoch for the
i
-th client,
and
\mathbf{w}_{k,0}^{(i)}=\mathbf{w}_{k}
is the initial local parameters for the
i
-th client.
The direction
\mathbf{u}_{k}
is given by the following equation with
the brevity notations
\mathbf{p}_{k}:=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}))
and
\mathbf{q}_{k}:=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}))
,
and we introduce
\mathbf{r}_{k}:=\mathbf{p}_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}+\mathbf{q}_{k}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{k}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}([\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}\mathds{1}_{k}+[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}])\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}

To help the analysis, we introduce the following notations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}=\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}],\quad\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}=\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}+\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

Step 1: Upperbound
\eta\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}
and Lowerbound
\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

Then we can decompose the direction as
\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}+(\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)})=\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-(\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)})-(\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)})
.

1.1: upperbound of
\eta\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}

Then by using
\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{x}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{y}\|^{2},\mathbb{E}[X]^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]

and the assumption of Lipschitz continuity
4.2
(
\|\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w})\|\leq L,\|\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w})\|\leq L
,
therefore
\|\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|\leq L,\left\|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\right\|\leq L

using
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}=1
and all
[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\geq 0
):

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}=\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\left\|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}+\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}(\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)})\right\|^{2}\leq\eta L^{2}K+\eta\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}

1.2: decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

We decompose the inner product by
introducing the Bregman divergence

D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\prime}||\mathbf{w}]:=f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\prime})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w})-\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}),\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\mathbf{w}\rangle\geq 0,D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\prime}||\mathbf{w}]:=g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\prime})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w})-\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}),\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\mathbf{w}\rangle\geq 0

for convex functions
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
from
4.1
,
therefore
\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=(f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})+D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}])\mathds{1}_{k}+(g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})+D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}])[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\bar{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}(f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}(g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

1.3: lowerbound of 1st and 2nd terms in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

Regarding the first and second terms in the above equation for the inner product,
using the definition of
F(\mathbf{w};\mathcal{I}_{k})=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}f_{i}(\mathbf{w};\mathcal{I}_{k})

and
G(\mathbf{w};\mathcal{I}_{k})=\max_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}g_{i}(\mathbf{w};\mathcal{I}_{k})
,
from the definition of
\mathbf{w}^{\ast}
such that
G(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq 0
in
Eq.

∗

.

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast};\mathcal{I}_{k})\leq F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}),\quad\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})\leq G(\mathbf{w}^{\ast};\mathcal{I}_{k})\leq G(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})=-|G(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})|\leq 0

Applying the deviation bound of softmax mean
Lemma

B.6

with
\alpha\geq\frac{2\ln m}{\epsilon^{\prime}}

and substituting
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}},\bm{\delta}\leftarrow\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}

and
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}},\bm{\delta}\leftarrow\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}
, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})-2\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{\infty}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})]-2\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{\infty}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})-\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{\infty}\right]\mathds{1}_{G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})>\frac{\epsilon}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2}-\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}\|_{\infty}\right)[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

Combining the above, we have the following lower bound for the first and second terms in the equation for the inner product:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}(f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-f_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}(g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-g_{i}(\mathbf{w}^{\ast}))[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}+\sum_{k\in[K]}\left[G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+|G(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})|\sum_{k\in[K]}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+\frac{\epsilon K}{2}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}+\epsilon}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}\mathds{1}_{k}-\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\|_{\infty}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}_{k};\mathcal{I}_{k})]\mathds{1}_{k}

1.4: upperbound of 3rd and 4th terms in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

Regarding the third term and fourth term in the equation for the inner product,
we noting the three-point Bregman divergence identity
Lemma

B.1

by substituting
\psi\leftarrow f_{i},g_{i}
,
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow\mathbf{w}_{k}
,
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathbf{w}^{\ast}
,
\hat{\mathbf{x}}\leftarrow\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
,
and using the fact that
D_{f_{i}}[\cdot||\cdot]\geq 0,D_{g_{i}}[\cdot||\cdot]\geq 0
from the definition of Bregman divergence
for convex functions
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
from
4.1
.

-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}]-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]\leq 0

-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}]-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]\leq 0

Using
(\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}=\gamma\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})\mathds{1}_{k}

and
(\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]=\gamma\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
from the update rule,
and applying Abel’s summation formula
\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\langle\bm{a}_{\tau},\mathbf{B}_{\tau}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{A}_{E},\mathbf{B}_{E-1}\rangle-\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\langle\mathbf{A}_{\tau+1},\bm{b}_{\tau}\rangle

with
\bm{a}_{0}=\vec{0},\mathbf{A}_{\tau}:=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\bm{a}_{\tau^{\prime}},\mathbf{B}_{\tau}:=\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\bm{b}_{\tau^{\prime}}

and substituting
\bm{a}_{\tau}\leftarrow\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\bm{b}_{\tau}\leftarrow\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})
, then
again applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lipschitz continuity
4.2
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau]}\left\langle\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2L},\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{L}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\left\langle\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2L},\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle-4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau+1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{2L},\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}(E-1)\mathds{1}_{k}+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\|\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)},\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle\mathds{1}_{k}

where the intermediate vector
\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)}:=-\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau+1]}\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{2L}

satisfies
\|\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)}\|\leq 1
by the assumption of Lipschitz continuity
4.2

and hence
\|\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\|\leq 2L
.
Similarly, by introducing the intermediate vector
\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)}:=-\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{\tau^{\prime}\in[\tau+1]}\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau^{\prime}}^{(i)})}{2L}

satisfies
\|\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)}\|\leq 1
by the assumption of Lipschitz continuity
4.2

and hence
\|\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})\|\leq 2L
, then:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\rangle[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}(E-1)[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\|[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle+4\gamma L(\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}})\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)},\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\right\rangle[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

We introduce the following notations,
\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}:=\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime(i)}\mathds{1}_{k}+\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{\prime\prime(i)}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

and
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}:=\frac{\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}\mathds{1}_{k}+\frac{\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\bm{\zeta}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)})}{2\sigma_{g}/\sqrt{B_{g}}}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]
.
Summing over
\tau\in[E]
and
i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}

and noting
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}=1
and
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}=1
,
the upperbound of the 3rd and 4th terms in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle
is given by:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{p}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{f_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right]\mathds{1}_{k}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{q}_{k}]_{i}\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left[-D_{g_{i}}[\mathbf{w}^{\ast}||\mathbf{w}_{k}]+\langle\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k})-\nabla g_{i}(\mathbf{w}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}),\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle\right][1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\gamma L^{2}(E-1)K+\frac{4\gamma L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|+\frac{4\gamma L\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E-1]}\tau\left\langle\mathbf{e}_{k,\tau}^{(i)},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

1.5: rewriting the 5th term in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

We introduce the the direction vector
\mathbf{d}_{k}:=\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\ast}-\mathbf{w}_{k}}{D}

which satisfies
\|\mathbf{d}_{k}\|\leq 1
by the assumption of finite diameter
D

of the parameter space
\Theta

4.3
.
From the definition of
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}
, we have:

\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}=-\frac{2\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}

Therefore, the 5th term in the decomposition of inner product
\sum_{k\in[K]}\langle\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle

can be rewritten as:

\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\langle\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}^{\ast}\rangle=\frac{2D\sigma_{g}}{\sqrt{B_{g}}}\cdot\frac{1}{E}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\left\langle\mathbf{d}_{k},\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\rangle

1.6: rewriting
\|\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}
in the upper bound of
\frac{\eta}{2}\sum_{k\in[K]}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}\|^{2}

By using the above equation with
\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)},\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}
,
the term in the upper bound of

\eta\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\|\mathbf{u}_{k}^{(i)}-\mathbf{\tilde{u}}_{k}^{(i)}\|^{2}=\frac{4\eta\sigma^{2}_{g}}{B_{g}}\cdot\frac{1}{E^{2}}\sum_{k\in[K]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\left\|\sum_{\tau\in[E]}\mathbf{z}_{k,\tau}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}

Step 2: Upperbound
\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}[F(\mathbf{w}_{k})-F(\mathbf{w}^{\ast})]\mathds{1}_{k}
and
\sum\limits_{k\in[K]}G(\mathbf{w}_{k})\mathds{1}_{k}

We introduce a filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}}
to track the information up to time

t:=\text{ind}(k,i,\tau)\equiv k\cdot nE+i\cdot E+\tau
and

\mathcal{F}_{t}:=\sigma((\bm{\zeta}_{k^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}}^{(i^{\prime})})_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k^{\prime}},\text{ind}(k^{\prime},i^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})<t},(\bm{\xi}_{k^{\prime}}^{(i^{\prime})})_{i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{k^{\prime}},\text{ind}(k^{\prime},0,0)\leq t},(\mathcal{I}_{k^{\prime}})_{\text{ind}(k^{\prime},0,0)\leq t})
with
\mathcal{F}_{-1}=\{\emptyset,\Omega\}
.
These introduced notations satisfy the following:

1. the indicator
\mathds{1}_{k}\equiv\mathds{1}_{G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable;
(from the definition of
G_{k}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k})=m(\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}),\alpha)
)

2. the softmax weights
\mathbf{r}_{k}
such that
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}=1

and
[\mathbf{r}_{k}]_{i}\geq 0
and is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable; (from the defintion of
\mathbf{r}_{k}
)

\mathbf{r}_{k}\equiv\mathbf{p}_{k}\mathds{1}_{k}+\mathbf{q}_{k}[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]=\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}))\mathds{1}_{k}+\mathrm{softmax}(\alpha\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{w}_{k},\bm{\xi}_{k}))[1-\mathds{1}_{k}]

3. the direction vectors
\mathbf{d}_{k}
and
\mathbf{e}_{k}^{(i)}
such that
\|\mathbf{d}_{k}\|\leq 1

(from the definition of

and the assumption of finite diameter of the parameter space
\Theta
